========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I guess... what made me finally bring it up and come in is that for the past few weeks, my eating has just been totally off. Like, I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to. I just kind of forget to eat. And then at dinner, I just sit there and pick at my food, even when my wife cooks. 

Which is crazy because she’s a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti—we actually started making it back when we were both taking classes at the community college, before I stopped going. It has like three kinds of cheese in it, and she gets this specific sausage from the butcher down the street. The butcher shop is actually closing next month, which is a real shame because it's been there for like forty years. The owner is retiring. 

But anyway, usually I'd eat half the pan of that ziti myself. Lately though, I just push it around my plate. And now my jeans are starting to feel really loose. It's frustrating because I just bought this pair, and finding jeans that fit right is a total nightmare now. They always change the cuts, you know? You find a brand you like and then boom, they discontinue it. But, uh... yeah, so the food and the clothes not fitting thing is mostly why I'm here.

No, not at all. I mean, I've never really been a gym guy or someone who counts calories. I'm not exactly sure how many pounds, honestly. I don't really weigh myself. But what made me bring it up is that for the past few weeks, my eating has just been way off. Like, I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then when my wife makes dinner, I just kind of pick at it. Which is a shame because she's a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti, it's her mom's recipe. Her mom is from New Jersey, right outside of Newark, and she always says the secret is in the ricotta cheese. You have to get the whole milk kind, not the part-skim stuff, or it gets watery. I actually tried making it once myself when she was working late, but I accidentally bought cottage cheese instead of ricotta. The containers look exactly the same if you're not paying attention! It was a total disaster, the texture was all wrong. We ended up just ordering pizza from that place down the street. Their crust is a little too thin for my liking, honestly. I prefer a thicker crust, almost like Detroit style. There's a place downtown that does Detroit style, but parking down there is a nightmare. You have to pay like twenty bucks just to park in a garage for an hour. Anyway, yeah, I've just been picking at my food, and I noticed the other day that my jeans are starting to feel pretty loose around the waist.

Like I said, I don't really know about percentages or numbers since I don't step on a scale. But like I was just telling you, my eating has just been really off for the past few weeks. I swear they sell the defective ones, like where the pockets are sewn just a quarter-inch too low or something. My brother-in-law works in retail and he says that's exactly what they do. He doesn't sell clothes, though, he sells electronics at Best Buy. He gets this crazy employee discount. He just bought a 75-inch TV for like half price, which is insane. I don't even know where you'd put a TV that big. Our living room is way too narrow, if you sat on the couch you'd be practically touching the screen with your nose. But anyway, yeah... I don't know if it's exactly five percent of my body weight, but I'm definitely shrinking out of my pants because I'm just not eating.

No, no, I haven't lost interest in it. I still pick it up. It's just... most days lately I wake up with this really heavy, flat feeling. Like the whole apartment is just too quiet, you know? And even when I do pick up the guitar, it doesn't really lift me up the way it usually does. I usually play a lot of blues stuff, like Stevie Ray Vaughan. He played a Stratocaster too, actually, but his was super beat up. He called it "Number One." I read a biography about him once, and he used these really thick strings, like bass strings almost, which is why his tone was so huge. I tried putting thicker strings on my guitar once, but it completely messed up the intonation and I had to take it to this guy across town to get it set up again. He works out of his basement, which is kind of weird, but he does great work. His basement is totally soundproofed, like with those foam egg carton things on the walls. I always wondered if those actually work or if they're just for show. My neighbor has a drum kit and I can hear him practicing every Tuesday night, so maybe I should suggest he get some of that foam. He's not very good, honestly. He mostly just plays the same beat over and over. But yeah, the guitar just isn't making me feel better right now, even though I still want to play it.

Dave actually just moved to Colorado, which is crazy because he hates the cold. He bought this huge truck to drive out there, an F-250 I think. Gas mileage on those things is terrible, though. I don't know how anyone affords to fill up a tank that size, especially with gas prices doing what they're doing. Every time I drive past the station on the corner, it's up another ten cents. I just put it right back down.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you first noticed your eating habits changing?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how many pounds, honestly. I don't really weigh myself. But what made me bring it up is that for the past few weeks, my eating has just been way off. Like, I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then when my wife makes dinner, I just kind of pick at it. Which is a shame because she's a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti, it's her mom's recipe. Her mom is from New Jersey, right outside of Newark, and she always says the secret is in the ricotta cheese. You have to get the whole milk kind, not the part-skim stuff, or it gets watery. The American ones are like three times the price, and honestly, unless you're playing gigs every weekend, you don't really need it. I bought mine off Craigslist years ago from this guy who was moving to Austin to try and make it in the music scene down there. I tried taking those little purple pills for a while, but they made my stomach feel weird. He played a Stratocaster too, actually, but his was super beat up. I tried putting thicker strings on my guitar once, but it completely messed up the intonation and I had to take it to this guy across town to get it set up again. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, honestly, right now... not really. I can't really think of anything that I still enjoy doing. It's like everything just feels like a chore, you know? Even watching TV feels like too much effort. We used to watch a lot of documentaries, my wife and I. We watched this whole series on deep-sea creatures a while back, which was wild. The things that live down there look like aliens. There's this one fish, the anglerfish, that has a glowing thing on its head to attract prey because it's completely pitch black down there. It's crazy to think about how much of the ocean is just unexplored. I read somewhere that we know more about the surface of the moon than we do about the bottom of the ocean. Which makes sense, I guess, since you can't exactly just send a submarine down to the deepest parts without it getting crushed by the pressure. They had to build this special sub just for James Cameron to go down to the Mariana Trench. He's the guy who directed Titanic, you know. I actually saw Titanic in theaters when it came out, it was a huge deal. The line wrapped around the building. But yeah, no, I don't really have anything I'm enjoying right now. Everything just feels kind of empty.

Anyway, yeah, I've just been picking at my food, and I noticed the other day that my jeans are starting to feel pretty loose around the waist.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't really know about percentages or numbers since I don't step on a scale. But like I was just telling you, my eating has just been really off for the past few weeks. I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even trying to, and then just pick at whatever my wife cooks for dinner. And my jeans are definitely getting noticeably loose. Actually, I buy all my jeans from this outlet mall up north, the one off the interstate. Have you ever been up there? It's massive. They have this huge Levi's store, but the sizing is always so inconsistent at those outlet places. I swear they sell the defective ones, like where the pockets are sewn just a quarter-inch too low or something. My brother-in-law works in retail and he says that's exactly what they do. He doesn't sell clothes, though, he sells electronics at Best Buy. He gets this crazy employee discount. He just bought a 75-inch TV for like half price, which is insane. I don't even know where you'd put a TV that big. Our living room is way too narrow, if you sat on the couch you'd be practically touching the screen with your nose. But anyway, yeah... I don't know if it's exactly five percent of my body weight, but I'm definitely shrinking out of my pants because I'm just not eating.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find yourself having no interest in?\n\nPatient: I mean, not really? Like I mentioned before, I still try to play my guitar, even though it doesn't make me feel better right now. But I still want to play it, you know? I actually have a Fender Stratocaster, the Mexican-made one, not the American one. The American ones are like three times the price, and honestly, unless you're playing gigs every weekend, you don't really need it. I bought mine off Craigslist years ago from this guy who was moving to Austin to try and make it in the music scene down there. Austin is supposed to be amazing for live music, they call it the live music capital of the world or something. My wife went there for a bachelorette party once and she said they just ate barbecue for like three days straight. She brought back this dry rub that we use on chicken sometimes, but it's a little too spicy for me. I get heartburn really easily, especially if I eat anything with too much cayenne pepper. I tried taking those little purple pills for a while, but they made my stomach feel weird. So anyway, no, I don't think I've lost interest in things, I still want to do my normal stuff, it just... feels different right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in playing your guitar?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't lost interest in it. I still pick it up. It's just... most days lately I wake up with this really heavy, flat feeling. Like the whole apartment is just too quiet, you know? And even when I do pick up the guitar, it doesn't really lift me up the way it usually does. I usually play a lot of blues stuff, like Stevie Ray Vaughan. He played a Stratocaster too, actually, but his was super beat up. He called it \"Number One.\" I read a biography about him once, and he used these really thick strings, like bass strings almost, which is why his tone was so huge. I tried putting thicker strings on my guitar once, but it completely messed up the intonation and I had to take it to this guy across town to get it set up again. He works out of his basement, which is kind of weird, but he does great work. His basement is totally soundproofed, like with those foam egg carton things on the walls. I always wondered if those actually work or if they're just for show. My neighbor has a drum kit and I can hear him practicing every Tuesday night, so maybe I should suggest he get some of that foam. He's not very good, honestly. He mostly just plays the same beat over and over. But yeah, the guitar just isn't making me feel better right now, even though I still want to play it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find interesting?\n\nPatient: I mean, honestly, right now... not really. I can't really think of anything that I still enjoy doing. It's like everything just feels like a chore, you know? Even watching TV feels like too much effort. We used to watch a lot of documentaries, my wife and I. We watched this whole series on deep-sea creatures a while back, which was wild. The things that live down there look like aliens. There's this one fish, the anglerfish, that has a glowing thing on its head to attract prey because it's completely pitch black down there. It's crazy to think about how much of the ocean is just unexplored. I read somewhere that we know more about the surface of the moon than we do about the bottom of the ocean. Which makes sense, I guess, since you can't exactly just send a submarine down to the deepest parts without it getting crushed by the pressure. They had to build this special sub just for James Cameron to go down to the Mariana Trench. He's the guy who directed Titanic, you know. I actually saw Titanic in theaters when it came out, it was a huge deal. The line wrapped around the building. But yeah, no, I don't really have anything I'm enjoying right now. Everything just feels kind of empty.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't know if it's ten pounds exactly, I don't really weigh myself. But what made me bring it up is that for the past few weeks my eating has just been totally off. Like, I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then I just pick at dinner, even when my wife cooks. She made her lasagna the other night, which is usually my absolute favorite. She uses those no-boil noodles, which I used to think were kind of cheating, but they actually bake up really well if you use enough sauce. We get the sauce in those massive glass jars from Costco. Costco is an absolute nightmare on the weekends, though, you can't even find a parking spot. I saw a guy back right into a shopping cart corral there once, completely dented his bumper, and he just drove off like nothing happened. People are crazy, you know? But anyway, yeah, I must be dropping some weight because my jeans are definitely starting to feel loose.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... what made me finally bring it up and come in is that for the past few weeks, my eating has just been totally off. Like, I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to. I just kind of forget to eat. And then at dinner, I just sit there and pick at my food, even when my wife cooks. \n\nWhich is crazy because she’s a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti—we actually started making it back when we were both taking classes at the community college, before I stopped going. It has like three kinds of cheese in it, and she gets this specific sausage from the butcher down the street. The butcher shop is actually closing next month, which is a real shame because it's been there for like forty years. The owner is retiring. \n\nBut anyway, usually I'd eat half the pan of that ziti myself. Lately though, I just push it around my plate. And now my jeans are starting to feel really loose. It's frustrating because I just bought this pair, and finding jeans that fit right is a total nightmare now. They always change the cuts, you know? You find a brand you like and then boom, they discontinue it. But, uh... yeah, so the food and the clothes not fitting thing is mostly why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I mean, I've never really been a gym guy or someone who counts calories. I tried counting calories once back in my twenties, but it was just so tedious entering everything into that little app on my phone. And honestly, I'm not great with phones anyway. I just had to upgrade mine because the battery kept dying at like 40 percent, and the guy at the store tried to sell me all these accessories I didn't need. They always do that, right? They see you coming and try to tack on a case and a screen protector and a car charger. I just wanted a phone that actually turns on when I need it to. But no, I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything like that. It's just happening.\n\nDoctor: Has something stressful or upsetting happened to you recently?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, nothing stands out. Life's just been pretty normal lately. Well, as normal as it can be when you're just hanging around the house all day. My wife has been working a lot, she's a dental hygienist, and they've been short-staffed because one of the other hygienists went on maternity leave. Actually, she had twins, which is wild. I can't even imagine having two babies at the exact same time. The stroller they bought for them is massive, it looks like a tank. It barely fits through a regular doorway. I saw a picture of it on Facebook. I don't really go on Facebook much anymore, though. It's just full of people arguing about politics or posting pictures of their breakfast. I prefer to just watch videos on YouTube, mostly like... woodworking stuff or guys restoring old tools. It's crazy how they can take a rusty old axe head and make it look brand new. But, uh... yeah, no big stressful events or anything like that. Just the usual day-to-day stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how much you enjoy your usual activities, like watching woodworking videos on YouTube?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess I still watch them. But honestly, I'll put one on and then just sort of stare at the screen without really paying attention. Which is weird because usually I'm fascinated by the whole process. Like, the other day I clicked on this video where a guy was restoring an old Stanley hand plane. Those things are incredible, you know? They don't make tools like they used to. My grandfather actually had a whole workbench full of them in his garage. He was a carpenter for like forty years, built half the houses in his neighborhood. The wood he used was always so solid. Now you go to the hardware store and everything is just MDF or that cheap pine that warps if you look at it wrong. I bought a couple of two-by-fours last month to try and fix a shelf in the laundry room, and I swear they were curved like a banana by the time I got them home. It's ridiculous how much they charge for lumber these days, too. But yeah... I don't know, I just haven't been getting into the videos like I used to. I just kind of let them play in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Most days lately I wake up and there's just this... heavy, flat feeling, you know? Like the whole apartment is just too quiet. I used to love the quiet in the mornings, especially before my wife gets up for work. She usually has to be at the clinic by 7:30, so she's up making coffee, and we have this old coffee maker that sounds like a jet engine taking off. I keep telling her we should buy one of those fancy espresso machines, the ones that grind the beans right there in the machine. My buddy Dave got one for his wedding, and he swears by it. Dave actually just moved to Colorado, which is crazy because he hates the cold. He bought this huge truck to drive out there, an F-250 I think. Gas mileage on those things is terrible, though. I don't know how anyone affords to fill up a tank that size, especially with gas prices doing what they're doing. Every time I drive past the station on the corner, it's up another ten cents. But anyway, yeah... even when I try to pick up my guitar to play a little, it doesn't really lift that heavy feeling at all. I just put it right back down.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you first noticed your eating habits changing?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how many pounds, honestly. I don't really weigh myself. But what made me bring it up is that for the past few weeks, my eating has just been way off. Like, I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then when my wife makes dinner, I just kind of pick at it. Which is a shame because she's a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti, it's her mom's recipe. Her mom is from New Jersey, right outside of Newark, and she always says the secret is in the ricotta cheese. You have to get the whole milk kind, not the part-skim stuff, or it gets watery. I actually tried making it once myself when she was working late, but I accidentally bought cottage cheese instead of ricotta. The containers look exactly the same if you're not paying attention! It was a total disaster, the texture was all wrong. We ended up just ordering pizza from that place down the street. Their crust is a little too thin for my liking, honestly. I prefer a thicker crust, almost like Detroit style. There's a place downtown that does Detroit style, but parking down there is a nightmare. You have to pay like twenty bucks just to park in a garage for an hour. They have this huge Levi's store, but the sizing is always so inconsistent at those outlet places. I swear they sell the defective ones, like where the pockets are sewn just a quarter-inch too low or something. My brother-in-law works in retail and he says that's exactly what they do. He's not very good, honestly. He mostly just plays the same beat over and over. But yeah, the guitar just isn't making me feel better right now, even though I still want to play it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find interesting?\n\nPatient: I mean, honestly, right now... not really. I can't really think of anything that I still enjoy doing. It's like everything just feels like a chore, you know? Even watching TV feels like too much effort. We used to watch a lot of documentaries, my wife and I. We watched this whole series on deep-sea creatures a while back, which was wild. The things that live down there look like aliens. There's this one fish, the anglerfish, that has a glowing thing on its head to attract prey because it's completely pitch black down there. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yeah, like I was just saying, she definitely notices when I just pick at the dinner she cooks. It's hard for her not to see it when my eating has been this off for the past few weeks and I'm skipping breakfast and lunch without even meaning to. She puts a lot of effort into dinner, too. We actually just got one of those air fryers because her sister wouldn't stop talking about hers. Have you tried one? It's supposed to make things crispy without oil, but honestly, it just seems like a tiny, really loud convection oven to me. 

I tried making sweet potato fries in it the other day and they just came out completely soggy. Sweet potatoes are weird like that, they never get as crispy as regular potatoes unless you coat them in cornstarch first. I saw that trick on a cooking show once. The host had this crazy handlebar mustache, like one of those old-timey strongmen at the circus. You know, the guys who wear the striped leotards and lift the giant round weights? I always wondered if those weights were actually heavy or if they were just hollow props made of wood or something. It seems like it would be a huge liability to have actual 500-pound iron balls rolling around a circus tent with all those kids everywhere. 

But, um... what was I saying? Oh, right, my wife noticing. Yeah, she probably notices the weight itself too, since, like I mentioned, my jeans are really starting to feel loose on me.

Like, I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then when my wife makes dinner, I just kind of pick at it. Which is a shame because she's a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti, it's her mom's recipe. Her mom is from New Jersey, right outside of Newark, and she always says the secret is in the ricotta cheese. You have to get the whole milk kind, not the part-skim stuff, or it gets watery. I swear they sell the defective ones, like where the pockets are sewn just a quarter-inch too low or something. My brother-in-law works in retail and he says that's exactly what they do. I don't even know where you'd put a TV that big. Our living room is way too narrow, if you sat on the couch you'd be practically touching the screen with your nose. But anyway, yeah... He doesn't sell clothes, though, he sells electronics at Best Buy. He gets this crazy employee discount. He just bought a 75-inch TV for like half price, which is insane. I don't even know where you'd put a TV that big. Our living room is way too narrow, if you sat on the couch you'd be practically touching the screen with your nose. But anyway, yeah... I don't know if it's exactly five percent of my body weight, but I'm definitely shrinking out of my pants because I'm just not eating.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find yourself having no interest in?\n\nPatient: I mean, not really? Like I mentioned before, I still try to play my guitar, even though it doesn't make me feel better right now. But I still want to play it, you know? I actually have a Fender Stratocaster, the Mexican-made one, not the American one. The American ones are like three times the price, and honestly, unless you're playing gigs every weekend, you don't really need it. I bought mine off Craigslist years ago from this guy who was moving to Austin to try and make it in the music scene down there. Austin is supposed to be amazing for live music, they call it the live music capital of the world or something. My wife went there for a bachelorette party once and she said they just ate barbecue for like three days straight. She brought back this dry rub that we use on chicken sometimes, but it's a little too spicy for me. I get heartburn really easily, especially if I eat anything with too much cayenne pepper. I tried taking those little purple pills for a while, but they made my stomach feel weird. So anyway, no, I don't think I've lost interest in things, I still want to do my normal stuff, it just... feels different right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in playing your guitar?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't lost interest in it. I still pick it up. It's just... most days lately I wake up with this really heavy, flat feeling. Like the whole apartment is just too quiet, you know? And even when I do pick up the guitar, it doesn't really lift me up the way it usually does. I usually play a lot of blues stuff, like Stevie Ray Vaughan. He played a Stratocaster too, actually, but his was super beat up. He called it \"Number One.\" I read a biography about him once, and he used these really thick strings, like bass strings almost, which is why his tone was so huge. I tried putting thicker strings on my guitar once, but it completely messed up the intonation and I had to take it to this guy across town to get it set up again. He works out of his basement, which is kind of weird, but he does great work. His basement is totally soundproofed, like with those foam egg carton things on the walls. I always wondered if those actually work or if they're just for show. My neighbor has a drum kit and I can hear him practicing every Tuesday night, so maybe I should suggest he get some of that foam. He's not very good, honestly. He mostly just plays the same beat over and over. But yeah, the guitar just isn't making me feel better right now, even though I still want to play it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find interesting?\n\nPatient: I mean, honestly, right now... not really. I can't really think of anything that I still enjoy doing. It's like everything just feels like a chore, you know? Even watching TV feels like too much effort. We used to watch a lot of documentaries, my wife and I. We watched this whole series on deep-sea creatures a while back, which was wild. The things that live down there look like aliens. There's this one fish, the anglerfish, that has a glowing thing on its head to attract prey because it's completely pitch black down there. It's crazy to think about how much of the ocean is just unexplored. I read somewhere that we know more about the surface of the moon than we do about the bottom of the ocean. Which makes sense, I guess, since you can't exactly just send a submarine down to the deepest parts without it getting crushed by the pressure. They had to build this special sub just for James Cameron to go down to the Mariana Trench. He's the guy who directed Titanic, you know. I actually saw Titanic in theaters when it came out, it was a huge deal. The line wrapped around the building. But yeah, no, I don't really have anything I'm enjoying right now. Everything just feels kind of empty.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've lost more than 10 pounds since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: I don't know if it's ten pounds exactly, I don't really weigh myself. But what made me bring it up is that for the past few weeks my eating has just been totally off. Like, I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then I just pick at dinner, even when my wife cooks. She made her lasagna the other night, which is usually my absolute favorite. She uses those no-boil noodles, which I used to think were kind of cheating, but they actually bake up really well if you use enough sauce. We get the sauce in those massive glass jars from Costco. Costco is an absolute nightmare on the weekends, though, you can't even find a parking spot. I saw a guy back right into a shopping cart corral there once, completely dented his bumper, and he just drove off like nothing happened. People are crazy, you know? But anyway, yeah, I must be dropping some weight because my jeans are definitely starting to feel loose.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight loss is noticeable to other people, like your wife?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was just saying, she definitely notices when I just pick at the dinner she cooks. It's hard for her not to see it when my eating has been this off for the past few weeks and I'm skipping breakfast and lunch without even meaning to. She puts a lot of effort into dinner, too. We actually just got one of those air fryers because her sister wouldn't stop talking about hers. Have you tried one? It's supposed to make things crispy without oil, but honestly, it just seems like a tiny, really loud convection oven to me. \n\nI tried making sweet potato fries in it the other day and they just came out completely soggy. Sweet potatoes are weird like that, they never get as crispy as regular potatoes unless you coat them in cornstarch first. I saw that trick on a cooking show once. The host had this crazy handlebar mustache, like one of those old-timey strongmen at the circus. You know, the guys who wear the striped leotards and lift the giant round weights? I always wondered if those weights were actually heavy or if they were just hollow props made of wood or something. It seems like it would be a huge liability to have actual 500-pound iron balls rolling around a circus tent with all those kids everywhere. \n\nBut, um... what was I saying? I mean, probably? If they hadn't seen me in a few weeks, yeah, they'd probably see it. Like I said, my jeans are getting really loose, and I've just been skipping breakfast and lunch without even realizing it. And then I just pick at whatever my wife makes for dinner. We actually had some friends over for dinner a couple of months ago, before all this started. They brought this bottle of wine that was supposed to be from some boutique vineyard in California. I don't really know much about wine, honestly. To me, it all kind of tastes the same unless it's really sweet. I prefer beer, usually. But not the heavy craft beers that taste like pine needles. IPAs are everywhere now, and I just don't get the appeal. Why would you want to drink something that tastes bitter on purpose? It's like drinking black coffee, which I also can't do. I need at least a little bit of cream in it. My dad used to drink his coffee black as night, said it put hair on his chest. He worked construction for thirty years, so I guess he needed the caffeine. He used to wake up at 4 AM every single day. I could never do that. 

But yeah, if someone saw me now compared to a few weeks ago, they'd probably notice I've dropped some weight since my eating has been so off.

Like, I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to. I just kind of forget to eat. And then at dinner, I just sit there and pick at my food, even when my wife cooks. \n\nWhich is crazy because she’s a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti—we actually started making it back when we were both taking classes at the community college, before I stopped going. It's frustrating because I just bought this pair, and finding jeans that fit right is a total nightmare now. They always change the cuts, you know? You find a brand you like and then boom, they discontinue it. But, uh... yeah, so the food and the clothes not fitting thing is mostly why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I mean, I've never really been a gym guy or someone who counts calories. I tried counting calories once back in my twenties, but it was just so tedious entering everything into that little app on my phone. And honestly, I'm not great with phones anyway. I just had to upgrade mine because the battery kept dying at like 40 percent, and the guy at the store tried to sell me all these accessories I didn't need. They always do that, right? They see you coming and try to tack on a case and a screen protector and a car charger. I just wanted a phone that actually turns on when I need it to. But no, I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything like that. It's just happening.\n\nDoctor: Has something stressful or upsetting happened to you recently?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, nothing stands out. Life's just been pretty normal lately. Well, as normal as it can be when you're just hanging around the house all day. My wife has been working a lot, she's a dental hygienist, and they've been short-staffed because one of the other hygienists went on maternity leave. Actually, she had twins, which is wild. I can't even imagine having two babies at the exact same time. The stroller they bought for them is massive, it looks like a tank. It barely fits through a regular doorway. I saw a picture of it on Facebook. I don't really go on Facebook much anymore, though. It's just full of people arguing about politics or posting pictures of their breakfast. I prefer to just watch videos on YouTube, mostly like... woodworking stuff or guys restoring old tools. It's crazy how they can take a rusty old axe head and make it look brand new. But, uh... yeah, no big stressful events or anything like that. Just the usual day-to-day stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how much you enjoy your usual activities, like watching woodworking videos on YouTube?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess I still watch them. But honestly, I'll put one on and then just sort of stare at the screen without really paying attention. Which is weird because usually I'm fascinated by the whole process. Like, the other day I clicked on this video where a guy was restoring an old Stanley hand plane. Those things are incredible, you know? They don't make tools like they used to. My grandfather actually had a whole workbench full of them in his garage. He was a carpenter for like forty years, built half the houses in his neighborhood. The wood he used was always so solid. Now you go to the hardware store and everything is just MDF or that cheap pine that warps if you look at it wrong. I bought a couple of two-by-fours last month to try and fix a shelf in the laundry room, and I swear they were curved like a banana by the time I got them home. It's ridiculous how much they charge for lumber these days, too. But yeah... I don't know, I just haven't been getting into the videos like I used to. I just kind of let them play in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Most days lately I wake up and there's just this... heavy, flat feeling, you know? Like the whole apartment is just too quiet. I used to love the quiet in the mornings, especially before my wife gets up for work. She usually has to be at the clinic by 7:30, so she's up making coffee, and we have this old coffee maker that sounds like a jet engine taking off. I keep telling her we should buy one of those fancy espresso machines, the ones that grind the beans right there in the machine. My buddy Dave got one for his wedding, and he swears by it. Dave actually just moved to Colorado, which is crazy because he hates the cold. He bought this huge truck to drive out there, an F-250 I think. Gas mileage on those things is terrible, though. I don't know how anyone affords to fill up a tank that size, especially with gas prices doing what they're doing. Every time I drive past the station on the corner, it's up another ten cents. But anyway, yeah... even when I try to pick up my guitar to play a little, it doesn't really lift that heavy feeling at all. I just put it right back down.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you first noticed your eating habits changing?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how many pounds, honestly. I don't really weigh myself. But what made me bring it up is that for the past few weeks, my eating has just been way off. Like, I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then when my wife makes dinner, I just kind of pick at it. Which is a shame because she's a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti, it's her mom's recipe. Her mom is from New Jersey, right outside of Newark, and she always says the secret is in the ricotta cheese. You have to get the whole milk kind, not the part-skim stuff, or it gets watery. I actually tried making it once myself when she was working late, but I accidentally bought cottage cheese instead of ricotta. The containers look exactly the same if you're not paying attention! It was a total disaster, the texture was all wrong. We ended up just ordering pizza from that place down the street. Their crust is a little too thin for my liking, honestly. I prefer a thicker crust, almost like Detroit style. There's a place downtown that does Detroit style, but parking down there is a nightmare. You have to pay like twenty bucks just to park in a garage for an hour. Anyway, yeah, I've just been picking at my food, and I noticed the other day that my jeans are starting to feel pretty loose around the waist.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't really know about percentages or numbers since I don't step on a scale. But like I was just telling you, my eating has just been really off for the past few weeks. I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even trying to, and then just pick at whatever my wife cooks for dinner. And my jeans are definitely getting noticeably loose. Actually, I buy all my jeans from this outlet mall up north, the one off the interstate. Have you ever been up there? It's massive. They have this huge Levi's store, but the sizing is always so inconsistent at those outlet places. I swear they sell the defective ones, like where the pockets are sewn just a quarter-inch too low or something. My brother-in-law works in retail and he says that's exactly what they do. He doesn't sell clothes, though, he sells electronics at Best Buy. He gets this crazy employee discount. He just bought a 75-inch TV for like half price, which is insane. I don't even know where you'd put a TV that big. Our living room is way too narrow, if you sat on the couch you'd be practically touching the screen with your nose. But anyway, yeah... I don't know if it's exactly five percent of my body weight, but I'm definitely shrinking out of my pants because I'm just not eating.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find yourself having no interest in?\n\nPatient: I mean, not really? Like I mentioned before, I still try to play my guitar, even though it doesn't make me feel better right now. But I still want to play it, you know? I actually have a Fender Stratocaster, the Mexican-made one, not the American one. The American ones are like three times the price, and honestly, unless you're playing gigs every weekend, you don't really need it. I bought mine off Craigslist years ago from this guy who was moving to Austin to try and make it in the music scene down there. Austin is supposed to be amazing for live music, they call it the live music capital of the world or something. My wife went there for a bachelorette party once and she said they just ate barbecue for like three days straight. She brought back this dry rub that we use on chicken sometimes, but it's a little too spicy for me. I get heartburn really easily, especially if I eat anything with too much cayenne pepper. I tried taking those little purple pills for a while, but they made my stomach feel weird. So anyway, no, I don't think I've lost interest in things, I still want to do my normal stuff, it just... feels different right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in playing your guitar?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't lost interest in it. I still pick it up. It's just... most days lately I wake up with this really heavy, flat feeling. Like the whole apartment is just too quiet, you know? And even when I do pick up the guitar, it doesn't really lift me up the way it usually does. I usually play a lot of blues stuff, like Stevie Ray Vaughan. He played a Stratocaster too, actually, but his was super beat up. He called it \"Number One.\" I read a biography about him once, and he used these really thick strings, like bass strings almost, which is why his tone was so huge. I tried putting thicker strings on my guitar once, but it completely messed up the intonation and I had to take it to this guy across town to get it set up again. He works out of his basement, which is kind of weird, but he does great work. His basement is totally soundproofed, like with those foam egg carton things on the walls. I always wondered if those actually work or if they're just for show. My neighbor has a drum kit and I can hear him practicing every Tuesday night, so maybe I should suggest he get some of that foam. He's not very good, honestly. He mostly just plays the same beat over and over. But yeah, the guitar just isn't making me feel better right now, even though I still want to play it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find interesting?\n\nPatient: I mean, honestly, right now... not really. I can't really think of anything that I still enjoy doing. It's like everything just feels like a chore, you know? Even watching TV feels like too much effort. We used to watch a lot of documentaries, my wife and I. We watched this whole series on deep-sea creatures a while back, which was wild. The things that live down there look like aliens. There's this one fish, the anglerfish, that has a glowing thing on its head to attract prey because it's completely pitch black down there. It's crazy to think about how much of the ocean is just unexplored. I read somewhere that we know more about the surface of the moon than we do about the bottom of the ocean. Which makes sense, I guess, since you can't exactly just send a submarine down to the deepest parts without it getting crushed by the pressure. They had to build this special sub just for James Cameron to go down to the Mariana Trench. He's the guy who directed Titanic, you know. I actually saw Titanic in theaters when it came out, it was a huge deal. The line wrapped around the building. But yeah, no, I don't really have anything I'm enjoying right now. Everything just feels kind of empty.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've lost more than 10 pounds since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: I don't know if it's ten pounds exactly, I don't really weigh myself. But what made me bring it up is that for the past few weeks my eating has just been totally off. Like, I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then I just pick at dinner, even when my wife cooks. She made her lasagna the other night, which is usually my absolute favorite. She uses those no-boil noodles, which I used to think were kind of cheating, but they actually bake up really well if you use enough sauce. We get the sauce in those massive glass jars from Costco. Costco is an absolute nightmare on the weekends, though, you can't even find a parking spot. I saw a guy back right into a shopping cart corral there once, completely dented his bumper, and he just drove off like nothing happened. People are crazy, you know? But anyway, yeah, I must be dropping some weight because my jeans are definitely starting to feel loose.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight loss is noticeable to other people, like your wife?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was just saying, she definitely notices when I just pick at the dinner she cooks. We actually just got one of those air fryers because her sister wouldn't stop talking about hers. Have you tried one? It's supposed to make things crispy without oil, but honestly, it just seems like a tiny, really loud convection oven to me. \n\nI tried making sweet potato fries in it the other day and they just came out completely soggy. Sweet potatoes are weird like that, they never get as crispy as regular potatoes unless you coat them in cornstarch first. I saw that trick on a cooking show once. The host had this crazy handlebar mustache, like one of those old-timey strongmen at the circus. You know, the guys who wear the striped leotards and lift the giant round weights? I always wondered if those weights were actually heavy or if they were just hollow props made of wood or something. It seems like it would be a huge liability to have actual 500-pound iron balls rolling around a circus tent with all those kids everywhere. \n\nBut, um... what was I saying? Oh, right, my wife noticing. Yeah, she probably notices the weight itself too, since, like I mentioned, my jeans are really starting to feel loose on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone who hasn't seen you in a while would notice that you've lost weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, probably? And then I just pick at whatever my wife makes for dinner. We actually had some friends over for dinner a couple of months ago, before all this started. They brought this bottle of wine that was supposed to be from some boutique vineyard in California. I don't really know much about wine, honestly. To me, it all kind of tastes the same unless it's really sweet. I prefer beer, usually. But not the heavy craft beers that taste like pine needles. IPAs are everywhere now, and I just don't get the appeal. Why would you want to drink something that tastes bitter on purpose? It's like drinking black coffee, which I also can't do. I need at least a little bit of cream in it. My dad used to drink his coffee black as night, said it put hair on his chest. He worked construction for thirty years, so I guess he needed the caffeine. No, not at all. I wasn't trying to diet or anything. Like I've been saying, the whole reason I even brought it up is just because my eating has been so off for the past few weeks. I'm skipping breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then picking at dinner, and now my jeans are loose. It's actually really annoying because I hate buying new jeans. Finding the right fit is impossible now because everything is "slim fit" or "athletic taper." I tried on a pair at the mall last year and I couldn't even get my calves through the legs. Who are they making these pants for? You have to have toothpicks for legs. 

The mall is so depressing nowadays anyway, half the stores are boarded up. The only things left are pretzel stands and those kiosks that sell cheap phone cases. The guys working there are so pushy, too. They literally step in front of you while you're walking to try and clean your screen or sell you a remote-control helicopter. It's like, no thanks, I can wipe my own phone on my shirt, you know? I did buy a pretzel last time I was there, though. Cinnamon sugar. It was okay, but they never give you enough icing to dip it in. 

But, um... yeah. I definitely wasn't trying to lose weight on purpose. It just kind of happened because I haven't been eating right.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, probably not healthy, yeah. Like I've been saying this whole time, the main reason I even brought the weight up is just because my eating has been so off for the past few weeks. Skipping breakfast and lunch by accident, picking at the dinners my wife makes, my jeans getting baggy... none of that feels like a healthy way to drop pounds. 

Healthy is a weird concept nowadays anyway, isn't it? Like, my wife takes all these expensive gummy vitamins now because she saw some influencer on TikTok pushing them. I really don't get TikTok. It's just people doing the exact same hand dances over and over in their kitchens. And their kitchens are always completely spotless! Like, who has bright white marble countertops and actually cooks? If you spill one drop of red wine or tomato sauce on that, it's stained forever. We have this ugly brown laminate in our kitchen. It was there when we moved in. The landlord said he'd update it before we signed the lease, but you know how landlords are. They promise you the moon and then completely ignore your texts when the sink backs up. Which actually happened to us on Thanksgiving two years ago. We had to call an emergency plumber on a holiday, and the guy charged an absolute fortune. He tracked mud all over the hallway carpet, too, and didn't even apologize. 

But, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, right, if the weight loss is healthy. Yeah, I guess probably not, since it's just happening because my appetite is gone and I'm not eating right.

I can't even imagine having two babies at the exact same time. The stroller they bought for them is massive, it looks like a tank. It barely fits through a regular doorway. I saw a picture of it on Facebook. I don't really go on Facebook much anymore, though. It's just full of people arguing about politics or posting pictures of their breakfast. I prefer to just watch videos on YouTube, mostly like... woodworking stuff or guys restoring old tools. It's crazy how they can take a rusty old axe head and make it look brand new. But, uh... yeah, no big stressful events or anything like that. Just the usual day-to-day stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how much you enjoy your usual activities, like watching woodworking videos on YouTube?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess I still watch them. But honestly, I'll put one on and then just sort of stare at the screen without really paying attention. Which is weird because usually I'm fascinated by the whole process. Like, the other day I clicked on this video where a guy was restoring an old Stanley hand plane. Those things are incredible, you know? They don't make tools like they used to. My grandfather actually had a whole workbench full of them in his garage. He was a carpenter for like forty years, built half the houses in his neighborhood. The wood he used was always so solid. Now you go to the hardware store and everything is just MDF or that cheap pine that warps if you look at it wrong. I bought a couple of two-by-fours last month to try and fix a shelf in the laundry room, and I swear they were curved like a banana by the time I got them home. It's ridiculous how much they charge for lumber these days, too. But yeah... I don't know, I just haven't been getting into the videos like I used to. I just kind of let them play in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Most days lately I wake up and there's just this... heavy, flat feeling, you know? Like the whole apartment is just too quiet. I used to love the quiet in the mornings, especially before my wife gets up for work. She usually has to be at the clinic by 7:30, so she's up making coffee, and we have this old coffee maker that sounds like a jet engine taking off. I keep telling her we should buy one of those fancy espresso machines, the ones that grind the beans right there in the machine. My buddy Dave got one for his wedding, and he swears by it. Dave actually just moved to Colorado, which is crazy because he hates the cold. He bought this huge truck to drive out there, an F-250 I think. Gas mileage on those things is terrible, though. I don't know how anyone affords to fill up a tank that size, especially with gas prices doing what they're doing. Every time I drive past the station on the corner, it's up another ten cents. But anyway, yeah... even when I try to pick up my guitar to play a little, it doesn't really lift that heavy feeling at all. I just put it right back down.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you first noticed your eating habits changing?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how many pounds, honestly. I don't really weigh myself. But what made me bring it up is that for the past few weeks, my eating has just been way off. Like, I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then when my wife makes dinner, I just kind of pick at it. Which is a shame because she's a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti, it's her mom's recipe. Her mom is from New Jersey, right outside of Newark, and she always says the secret is in the ricotta cheese. You have to get the whole milk kind, not the part-skim stuff, or it gets watery. I actually tried making it once myself when she was working late, but I accidentally bought cottage cheese instead of ricotta. The containers look exactly the same if you're not paying attention! It was a total disaster, the texture was all wrong. We ended up just ordering pizza from that place down the street. Their crust is a little too thin for my liking, honestly. I prefer a thicker crust, almost like Detroit style. There's a place downtown that does Detroit style, but parking down there is a nightmare. You have to pay like twenty bucks just to park in a garage for an hour. Anyway, yeah, I've just been picking at my food, and I noticed the other day that my jeans are starting to feel pretty loose around the waist.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't really know about percentages or numbers since I don't step on a scale. But like I was just telling you, my eating has just been really off for the past few weeks. I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even trying to, and then just pick at whatever my wife cooks for dinner. And my jeans are definitely getting noticeably loose. Actually, I buy all my jeans from this outlet mall up north, the one off the interstate. Have you ever been up there? It's massive. They have this huge Levi's store, but the sizing is always so inconsistent at those outlet places. I swear they sell the defective ones, like where the pockets are sewn just a quarter-inch too low or something. My brother-in-law works in retail and he says that's exactly what they do. He doesn't sell clothes, though, he sells electronics at Best Buy. He gets this crazy employee discount. He just bought a 75-inch TV for like half price, which is insane. I don't even know where you'd put a TV that big. Our living room is way too narrow, if you sat on the couch you'd be practically touching the screen with your nose. But anyway, yeah... I don't know if it's exactly five percent of my body weight, but I'm definitely shrinking out of my pants because I'm just not eating.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find yourself having no interest in?\n\nPatient: I mean, not really? Like I mentioned before, I still try to play my guitar, even though it doesn't make me feel better right now. But I still want to play it, you know? I bought mine off Craigslist years ago from this guy who was moving to Austin to try and make it in the music scene down there. Austin is supposed to be amazing for live music, they call it the live music capital of the world or something. My wife went there for a bachelorette party once and she said they just ate barbecue for like three days straight. She brought back this dry rub that we use on chicken sometimes, but it's a little too spicy for me. I get heartburn really easily, especially if I eat anything with too much cayenne pepper. I tried taking those little purple pills for a while, but they made my stomach feel weird. So anyway, no, I don't think I've lost interest in things, I still want to do my normal stuff, it just... feels different right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in playing your guitar?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't lost interest in it. He played a Stratocaster too, actually, but his was super beat up. I tried putting thicker strings on my guitar once, but it completely messed up the intonation and I had to take it to this guy across town to get it set up again. He works out of his basement, which is kind of weird, but he does great work. His basement is totally soundproofed, like with those foam egg carton things on the walls. I always wondered if those actually work or if they're just for show. My neighbor has a drum kit and I can hear him practicing every Tuesday night, so maybe I should suggest he get some of that foam. He's not very good, honestly. He mostly just plays the same beat over and over. But yeah, the guitar just isn't making me feel better right now, even though I still want to play it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find interesting?\n\nPatient: I mean, honestly, right now... not really. There's this one fish, the anglerfish, that has a glowing thing on its head to attract prey because it's completely pitch black down there. They had to build this special sub just for James Cameron to go down to the Mariana Trench. He's the guy who directed Titanic, you know. It's just people doing the exact same hand dances over and over in their kitchens. And their kitchens are always completely spotless! Like, who has bright white marble countertops and actually cooks? If you spill one drop of red wine or tomato sauce on that, it's stained forever. We have this ugly brown laminate in our kitchen. It was there when we moved in. The landlord said he'd update it before we signed the lease, but you know how landlords are. They promise you the moon and then completely ignore your texts when the sink backs up. Which actually happened to us on Thanksgiving two years ago. We had to call an emergency plumber on a holiday, and the guy charged an absolute fortune. He tracked mud all over the hallway carpet, too, and didn't even apologize. \n\nBut, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, right, if the weight loss is healthy. Yeah, I guess probably not, since it's just happening because my appetite is gone and I'm not eating right.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your lack of interest in activities like playing guitar or watching videos is related to your weight loss?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 31 / no_occupation / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressed_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Most days lately I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling, like the apartment is too quiet and even picking up my guitar doesn’t really lift me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know if they're related, really. It's more just... I don't know. Most days lately I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling, you know? Like the apartment is just too quiet. And even picking up my guitar doesn't really lift me out of it. 

I actually haven't changed the strings on that guitar in probably a year. You're supposed to change them every couple of months if you play a lot, but they're so expensive now. Everything is expensive. I went to buy a pack of strings at the local music shop, and the guy behind the counter tried to upsell me on these coated ones that supposedly last longer. He kept talking about the tone and the resonance and all this technical stuff. I just wanted regular strings. The guy had this huge beard, too, like one of those lumberjack beards. I don't know how guys eat with those things. I tried to grow a beard once, maybe like five years ago? It just came in all patchy and itchy. My wife hated it. She said I looked like a guy who sleeps on a bus bench. Which, honestly, she wasn't wrong. I shaved it off after like three weeks. 

But yeah, I don't know if the weight loss is connected to not wanting to play or whatever. It's just... I wake up feeling really flat and heavy, and nothing really seems worth doing.

Dave actually just moved to Colorado, which is crazy because he hates the cold. He bought this huge truck to drive out there, an F-250 I think. Gas mileage on those things is terrible, though. I don't know how anyone affords to fill up a tank that size, especially with gas prices doing what they're doing. Every time I drive past the station on the corner, it's up another ten cents. But anyway, yeah... even when I try to pick up my guitar to play a little, it doesn't really lift that heavy feeling at all. I just put it right back down.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you first noticed your eating habits changing?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how many pounds, honestly. I don't really weigh myself. But what made me bring it up is that for the past few weeks, my eating has just been way off. Like, I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then when my wife makes dinner, I just kind of pick at it. Which is a shame because she's a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti, it's her mom's recipe. Her mom is from New Jersey, right outside of Newark, and she always says the secret is in the ricotta cheese. You have to get the whole milk kind, not the part-skim stuff, or it gets watery. I actually tried making it once myself when she was working late, but I accidentally bought cottage cheese instead of ricotta. The containers look exactly the same if you're not paying attention! It was a total disaster, the texture was all wrong. We ended up just ordering pizza from that place down the street. Their crust is a little too thin for my liking, honestly. I prefer a thicker crust, almost like Detroit style. There's a place downtown that does Detroit style, but parking down there is a nightmare. You have to pay like twenty bucks just to park in a garage for an hour. Anyway, yeah, I've just been picking at my food, and I noticed the other day that my jeans are starting to feel pretty loose around the waist.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't really know about percentages or numbers since I don't step on a scale. But like I was just telling you, my eating has just been really off for the past few weeks. I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even trying to, and then just pick at whatever my wife cooks for dinner. And my jeans are definitely getting noticeably loose. Actually, I buy all my jeans from this outlet mall up north, the one off the interstate. Have you ever been up there? It's massive. They have this huge Levi's store, but the sizing is always so inconsistent at those outlet places. I swear they sell the defective ones, like where the pockets are sewn just a quarter-inch too low or something. My brother-in-law works in retail and he says that's exactly what they do. He doesn't sell clothes, though, he sells electronics at Best Buy. He gets this crazy employee discount. He just bought a 75-inch TV for like half price, which is insane. I don't even know where you'd put a TV that big. Our living room is way too narrow, if you sat on the couch you'd be practically touching the screen with your nose. But anyway, yeah... I don't know if it's exactly five percent of my body weight, but I'm definitely shrinking out of my pants because I'm just not eating.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find yourself having no interest in?\n\nPatient: I mean, not really? Like I mentioned before, I still try to play my guitar, even though it doesn't make me feel better right now. But I still want to play it, you know? I actually have a Fender Stratocaster, the Mexican-made one, not the American one. The American ones are like three times the price, and honestly, unless you're playing gigs every weekend, you don't really need it. I bought mine off Craigslist years ago from this guy who was moving to Austin to try and make it in the music scene down there. Austin is supposed to be amazing for live music, they call it the live music capital of the world or something. My wife went there for a bachelorette party once and she said they just ate barbecue for like three days straight. She brought back this dry rub that we use on chicken sometimes, but it's a little too spicy for me. I get heartburn really easily, especially if I eat anything with too much cayenne pepper. I tried taking those little purple pills for a while, but they made my stomach feel weird. So anyway, no, I don't think I've lost interest in things, I still want to do my normal stuff, it just... feels different right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in playing your guitar?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't lost interest in it. I still pick it up. It's just... most days lately I wake up with this really heavy, flat feeling. Like the whole apartment is just too quiet, you know? And even when I do pick up the guitar, it doesn't really lift me up the way it usually does. I usually play a lot of blues stuff, like Stevie Ray Vaughan. He played a Stratocaster too, actually, but his was super beat up. He called it \"Number One.\" I read a biography about him once, and he used these really thick strings, like bass strings almost, which is why his tone was so huge. I tried putting thicker strings on my guitar once, but it completely messed up the intonation and I had to take it to this guy across town to get it set up again. He works out of his basement, which is kind of weird, but he does great work. His basement is totally soundproofed, like with those foam egg carton things on the walls. I always wondered if those actually work or if they're just for show. My neighbor has a drum kit and I can hear him practicing every Tuesday night, so maybe I should suggest he get some of that foam. He's not very good, honestly. He mostly just plays the same beat over and over. But yeah, the guitar just isn't making me feel better right now, even though I still want to play it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and find interesting?\n\nPatient: I mean, honestly, right now... not really. I can't really think of anything that I still enjoy doing. It's like everything just feels like a chore, you know? Even watching TV feels like too much effort. We used to watch a lot of documentaries, my wife and I. We watched this whole series on deep-sea creatures a while back, which was wild. The things that live down there look like aliens. There's this one fish, the anglerfish, that has a glowing thing on its head to attract prey because it's completely pitch black down there. It's crazy to think about how much of the ocean is just unexplored. I read somewhere that we know more about the surface of the moon than we do about the bottom of the ocean. Which makes sense, I guess, since you can't exactly just send a submarine down to the deepest parts without it getting crushed by the pressure. They had to build this special sub just for James Cameron to go down to the Mariana Trench. He's the guy who directed Titanic, you know. I actually saw Titanic in theaters when it came out, it was a huge deal. The line wrapped around the building. But yeah, no, I don't really have anything I'm enjoying right now. Everything just feels kind of empty.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've lost more than 10 pounds since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: I don't know if it's ten pounds exactly, I don't really weigh myself. But what made me bring it up is that for the past few weeks my eating has just been totally off. Like, I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then I just pick at dinner, even when my wife cooks. She made her lasagna the other night, which is usually my absolute favorite. She uses those no-boil noodles, which I used to think were kind of cheating, but they actually bake up really well if you use enough sauce. We get the sauce in those massive glass jars from Costco. Costco is an absolute nightmare on the weekends, though, you can't even find a parking spot. I saw a guy back right into a shopping cart corral there once, completely dented his bumper, and he just drove off like nothing happened. People are crazy, you know? But anyway, yeah, I must be dropping some weight because my jeans are definitely starting to feel loose.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight loss is noticeable to other people, like your wife?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was just saying, she definitely notices when I just pick at the dinner she cooks. It's hard for her not to see it when my eating has been this off for the past few weeks and I'm skipping breakfast and lunch without even meaning to. She puts a lot of effort into dinner, too. We actually just got one of those air fryers because her sister wouldn't stop talking about hers. Have you tried one? It's supposed to make things crispy without oil, but honestly, it just seems like a tiny, really loud convection oven to me. \n\nI tried making sweet potato fries in it the other day and they just came out completely soggy. Sweet potatoes are weird like that, they never get as crispy as regular potatoes unless you coat them in cornstarch first. I saw that trick on a cooking show once. The host had this crazy handlebar mustache, like one of those old-timey strongmen at the circus. You know, the guys who wear the striped leotards and lift the giant round weights? I always wondered if those weights were actually heavy or if they were just hollow props made of wood or something. It seems like it would be a huge liability to have actual 500-pound iron balls rolling around a circus tent with all those kids everywhere. \n\nBut, um... what was I saying? They brought this bottle of wine that was supposed to be from some boutique vineyard in California. I don't really know much about wine, honestly. IPAs are everywhere now, and I just don't get the appeal. Why would you want to drink something that tastes bitter on purpose? It's like drinking black coffee, which I also can't do. I need at least a little bit of cream in it. My dad used to drink his coffee black as night, said it put hair on his chest. He worked construction for thirty years, so I guess he needed the caffeine. He used to wake up at 4 AM every single day. They literally step in front of you while you're walking to try and clean your screen or sell you a remote-control helicopter. It's like, no thanks, I can wipe my own phone on my shirt, you know? I definitely wasn't trying to lose weight on purpose. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, exactly. I feel like a broken record here, but I definitely wasn't trying to make it happen. Like I've been saying, the whole reason I even brought it up is just that for the past few weeks my eating has been completely off. I'll skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then I just pick at dinner, even when my wife cooked. And now my jeans are starting to feel loose. 

She actually made this huge baked ziti two nights ago, which is usually my favorite. She uses this specific ricotta cheese with a red lid, but she had to go to three different grocery stores just to find it because our local place is always out of stock. Grocery stores are a complete nightmare right now anyway. They keep moving all the items around. Like, why put the bread in aisle four one week and then move it to aisle nine the next? You end up walking in circles for twenty minutes just looking for a loaf of sourdough. And don't even get me started on those self-checkout machines. They replaced all the actual cashiers with these robots that just yell at you about an "unexpected item in the bagging area." It's like, it's a lemon, just let me buy it! I swear the machine is just waiting to freeze up so the one teenager working the whole section has to come over and scan his little barcode badge. 

But, um... yeah. I didn't try to lose the weight. It's just happening because my eating has been so messed up lately.

It's ridiculous how much they charge for lumber these days, too. But yeah... I don't know, I just haven't been getting into the videos like I used to. I just kind of let them play in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Most days lately I wake up and there's just this... heavy, flat feeling, you know? Like the whole apartment is just too quiet. She usually has to be at the clinic by 7:30, so she's up making coffee, and we have this old coffee maker that sounds like a jet engine taking off. I keep telling her we should buy one of those fancy espresso machines, the ones that grind the beans right there in the machine. My buddy Dave got one for his wedding, and he swears by it. Dave actually just moved to Colorado, which is crazy because he hates the cold. He bought this huge truck to drive out there, an F-250 I think. Gas mileage on those things is terrible, though. But anyway, yeah... even when I try to pick up my guitar to play a little, it doesn't really lift that heavy feeling at all. I just put it right back down.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you first noticed your eating habits changing?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how many pounds, honestly. Like, I'll completely skip breakfast and lunch without even meaning to, and then when my wife makes dinner, I just kind of pick at it. Which is a shame because she's a really good cook. She makes this incredible baked ziti, it's her mom's recipe. Her mom is from New Jersey, right outside of Newark, and she always says the secret is in the ricotta cheese. You have to get the whole milk kind, not the part-skim stuff, or it gets watery. I actually tried making it once myself when she was working late, but I accidentally bought cottage cheese instead of ricotta. The containers look exactly the same if you're not paying attention! It was a total disaster, the texture was all wrong. We ended up just ordering pizza from that place down the street. Their crust is a little too thin for my liking, honestly. I prefer a thicker crust, almost like Detroit style. There's a place downtown that does Detroit style, but parking down there is a nightmare. You have to pay like twenty bucks just to park in a garage for an hour. Anyway, yeah, I've just been picking at my food, and I noticed the other day that my jeans are starting to feel pretty loose around the waist.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't really know about percentages or numbers since I don't step on a scale. They have this huge Levi's store, but the sizing is always so inconsistent at those outlet places. The American ones are like three times the price, and honestly, unless you're playing gigs every weekend, you don't really need it. There's this one fish, the anglerfish, that has a glowing thing on its head to attract prey because it's completely pitch black down there.
